## Context

- The most-copied code shape: thousands of builtins. A change after the freeze touches all of them [R07: Brimstone's handle refactor].
- Depends on: H10 (CSS keeps Rust locals alive), H14 (frame layout: `Args` reads the host frame), H06 (`Value` wraps `NonZeroU64`), H19 (atoms are the property keys), H22 (realms, R5). Depended on by: the builtins (P2, P3), H13 (BuiltinId index list), H35 (unit link cells), the JIT and AOT host-call stub.

## Decision

Plan row (02 §6): `fn(cx, this, args, new_target) -> JsResult<Local<'s>>`. `Args` is a frame view; `Throw` is a ZST; no `&mut` to a heap object across a JS call. AOT code reaches builtins through the isolate table or the link table. The row said `-> JsResult<Value>` until the v1 draft put `Local` in place of `Value` (a rewrite of the row's result type before the freeze; P4-DOCS updated 02 in 6bebbb1, M0 wk4):

```rust
pub type HostFn = for<'s> fn(&mut Cx<'s>, Local<'s>, Args<'s>, Local<'s>) -> JsResult<Local<'s>>;
//                           cx           this       args (= fp)  new_target
```

| Type | What it is | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `Cx<'s>` | the context: ObjectOps, conversions, calls, `throw_*`; exposes no `&Runtime` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:509` |
| `Local<'s, T>` | the value itself, `repr(transparent)` over `Value`; `Copy`, `!Send`, `!Sync`; markers `JsValue` (default), `JsObject`, `JsFunction`, `JsArray`, `JsString`, `FlatStr`, `JsSymbol`, `JsKey`; checked downcasts, `From` upcasts | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/local.rs:147` |
| `Args<'s>` | one word, the host frame pointer: `len`, `get`, `this`, `new_target`, `callee`, `rest(i)` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:100` |
| `PropertyDescriptor<'s>` | §6.2.6, every field optional | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:229` |
| `ScratchList<'s>` | LIFO precise list over `ep9_heap::ScratchRoots` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:378` |
| `ArgsBuilder<'s>` | call arguments on the VM stack above the frame top, under the scratch top (no heap list) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:409` |
| `IteratorRecord<'s>` | an Iterator Record (§7.4.1): `iterator()`, `next_method()`, `is_done()`; stack-only (R2) because it holds `Local`s | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/iter.rs:30` |
| `PromiseCapability<'s>` | a PromiseCapability Record (§27.2.1.1): `promise`, `resolve`, `reject`; stack-only (R2) because it holds `Local`s | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/jobs.rs:78` |

**ObjectOps v0** (`Cx` methods, spec names): `get`, `get_v`, `set`, `has_property`, `has_own_property`, `get_own_property`, `define_own_property` (ValidateAndApplyPropertyDescriptor), `define_property_or_throw`, `create_data_property(_or_throw)`, `delete`, `delete_property_or_throw`, `own_property_keys` (a JS array: indices ascending, strings in creation order, symbols), `get/set_prototype_of`, `is_extensible`, `prevent_extensions`, `is_callable`, `is_constructor`, `is_array`. Conversions: `to_boolean`, `to_number`, `to_integer_or_infinity`, `to_int32`, `to_uint32`, `to_length`, `to_string`, `to_property_key`, `to_object`, `to_primitive`, `same_value`, `is_strictly_equal`. Spec helpers: `to/from_property_descriptor`, `create_list_from_array_like` (into an `ArgsBuilder`), `get_prototype_from_constructor`, `ordinary_create_from_constructor` (a `prototype` that is not an object falls back to the default intrinsic of GetFunctionRealm(constructor), §10.1.14 step 4). Creation: `new_object(_with_proto)`, `new_array`, `create_array_from_list`, `new_string(_utf16)`, `new_wrapper`, `new_symbol`, `intrinsic(IntrinsicId)`. P3 surface (P2-BTAB): `symbol_for`, `key_for`, `symbol_description`, `descriptive_string`, `this_primitive_value(v, PrimitiveType)`, `set_function_name`, `set_function_length`, `new_bound_function(target, this, args.rest(i))`, `has_intrinsic`, `initial_name(f) -> Option<Local<JsString>>` (the `[[InitialName]]` of a host function, from its FunctionInfo; `None` for bytecode and bound functions; an accessor's functions are named "get x" / "set x"; W4-P3FIX-INITIAL-NAME). Calls: `call`, `call_args(f, this, args.rest(i))`, `call_built`, `construct(f, args, new_target)` with any constructor as new.target, `construct_args`, `construct_built`. **Index access** (ABI-1, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/index.rs`): `get_index(o, k)`, `has_index(o, k)`, `set_index(o, k, v, throw)` and `delete_index(o, k, throw)` are Get, HasProperty, Set and `[[Delete]]` / DeletePropertyOrThrow with the key `! ToString(𝔽(k))` for a `u64` `k`. A dense element and a missing index need no key, so they make no atom; a stored integer key of an object with at most 64 own properties is found by a scan (the array index from the atom's hash field, or the digits); otherwise the key is interned and the ordinary path runs (`property::get_index` and its neighbours, `crates/ep9_vm/src/property.rs:1888`). A missing array index stops at the receiver while no prototype has an index key (`shape::proto_index_keys`). `poll_interrupt()` takes one interrupt budget unit and polls when it runs out (termination gives an uncatchable `Err(Throw)`): a builtin loop over indices has no back edge, so it calls it once per index. **Iterator protocol** (ABI-3, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/iter.rs`): `get_iterator(obj)` (GetIterator, sync; the async form comes later as an addition), `get_iterator_from_method(obj, method)`, `iterator_step_value(&mut record) -> Option<Local>` (a throw or done sets `[[Done]]`; one interrupt budget unit per step) and `iterator_close(&record, completion)` (a throw completion keeps its exception whatever `return` does). Accessor properties are built (P3-ACC). v0 limits: these throw "not implemented" until H18 element storage: an accessor on an array index, an array element whose attributes are not {w, e, c}, and a non-writable array `length` (W4-ACC-ELEM).

**Internal slots** (ABI-SLOTS, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/slots.rs`): `new_object_with_slots(proto, class, n)`, `slot(o, class, i)`, `set_slot(o, class, i, v)` and `object_class(v)`. The class decides what each slot index means, and its builtin module names the indices. Slots hold Values in a heap cell (plan rule 8), and property code never touches them. A slot index past the count panics.

**JIT and AOT** never call a `HostFn` (Rust ABI). They call one `extern "C"` host-call stub `(cx, fp) -> bits` that pushes the host frame and runs `call::call_host`; the builtin is named by its FunctionInfo or its `BuiltinId` (block 7 bits + index 9 bits) through the isolate table or a unit link cell (H35).

### Builtin tables (M0 wk4 P2)

```text
IsolateConfig { builtins: Some(&ep9_builtins::TABLE), .. }
Isolate::new
  builtins::check      both tables pass check_tables (cached per table)  -> IsolateError::BuiltinTable
  Runtime::new
  builtins::prepare    one dense FunctionInfo array + one root range of function slots per module
  realm::init          atoms; core intrinsics: %Object.prototype%, %Function.prototype% (VM builtin 0.0),
                       Array/String/Number/Boolean/Iterator/ArrayIterator/Error prototypes, the global
  builtins::install    VM table (block 0), then the embedder table, modules in order:
                       pass 1 every function object; pass 2 the intrinsics; pass 3 the properties
```

| Type | What it is | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `BuiltinId(u16)` | block (7 bits, < 128) + index (9 bits, < 512): definition `i` of the module with block `b`. Blocks are fixed per module; ids are append-only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:55` |
| `IntrinsicId` | 72 append-only ids: the core prototypes 0-16 (made by realm init; `SymbolPrototype` by `symbol::install`), the constructors and `%Math%` 17-31 (made by tables), `%StringIteratorPrototype%` 32 (made by the VM table), `%GeneratorFunction%`, its prototype, `%GeneratorPrototype%`, `%AsyncFunction%`, its prototype and `%eval%` 33-38 (the VM table), then `%Promise%`, `%Promise.prototype%`, `%AggregateError%` and `%AggregateError.prototype%` 39-42 (`ep9_builtins` promise.rs, M2-P4), `%Reflect%` 43 (`ep9_builtins` reflect.rs, M2-P5), `%Array.prototype%[@@unscopables]` 44 (the VM table natives.rs, M2-P5 VM-SMALL), and ids 45-63 declared in M3-P0 for the month-3 builders (P0-D3): `%AsyncGeneratorFunction%`, its prototype, `%AsyncGeneratorPrototype%`, `%AsyncIteratorPrototype%`, `%AsyncFromSyncIteratorPrototype%` 45-49 (the VM table, M3-P2 ASYNCGEN); `%Date%`, `%Date.prototype%` 50-51 (`ep9_builtins` date.rs, M3-P1); `%Map%`, `%Set%`, `%WeakMap%`, `%WeakSet%` and their prototypes 52-59, `%MapIteratorPrototype%`, `%SetIteratorPrototype%` 60-61 (collections.rs, M3-P1); `%JSON%` 62 (json.rs, M3-P1, an ordinary object with `%Object.prototype%`); `%ThrowTypeError%` 63 (the VM table, M3-P2 VM-ARGS step 2); and ids 64-71 declared in M4-P0 for the month-4 builders, made by none yet (plan v9 rule 18): `%Proxy%` 64 (M4-P4 PROXY1), `%RegExp%`, `%RegExp.prototype%`, `%RegExpStringIteratorPrototype%` 65-67 (M4-P2 RX0A, M4-P3 REGEXP1), `%WeakRef%`, `%WeakRef.prototype%`, `%FinalizationRegistry%`, `%FinalizationRegistry.prototype%` 68-71 (M4-P4 WEAKREF). Each maps to a runtime root, except `SymbolPrototype` (kept by `symbol.rs`) and ids 32-71, which are slots 0-39 of the isolate's builtin state (W4-NAT-STRITER-PROTO; `IntrinsicId::slot`) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:105` |
| `BuiltinDef` | `{name, length, kind: Method \| Getter \| Setter \| Constructor, f: HostFn}`; a getter is named "get x" | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:584` |
| `PropDef` | `{key, value, attrs}`. Key: `Atom` (the `atoms!` list), `Str` (interned at init), `Symbol` (well-known). Value: `Builtin(i)`, `Intrinsic`, `Const`, `Symbol`, `Alias(key)` (the value of a key defined earlier on the same object: one function object under two names), `Accessor {get, set}` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:810` |
| `BuiltinModule`, `IntrinsicDef`, `ObjectProps` | a module: name, block, `defs`, the intrinsics it makes (`Builtin(i)` or a new ordinary object), the properties per target intrinsic | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:890` |
| `BuiltinTable` | a name and its modules; `check_tables` checks a list of tables | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:969`, `:1015` |

`check_tables` rejects: a block ≥ 128 or used twice; more than 512 definitions; an intrinsic made twice, or a core one made other than as a VM builtin; a property on, or a reference to, an intrinsic nothing makes; a definition index out of range or of the wrong kind (`Builtin` needs a Method or Constructor, `Accessor` a Getter/Setter); an `Alias` of a key not defined earlier on the same object; a key defined twice on one object. A constructor that a table makes must get `prototype` ({} attributes) as its first table key, and must get one (W4-BTAB-CTOR-PROTO-FIRST). Install also refuses, in every build, a key the target already has (a function's own `length`/`name`, or anything realm init made), with `IsolateError::BuiltinTable` (W4-BTAB-INSTALL-NO-SECOND-KEY). Install errors (a well-known symbol or an accessor that does not exist yet) fail `Isolate::new` with a message naming the module and key.

**One function object per builtin and realm.** `builtins::function_object` makes it on first use and keeps it in the module's root slot (the first realm) or in the install list while a later realm installs (`builtins::begin_realm_install`), so an `Alias`, an intrinsic and a property name one object in each realm. Every realm's function object of a definition shares its FunctionInfo. The FunctionInfos sit in one boxed slice per module (no Box per function); `rt.infos` keeps only embedder host functions.

**Ids are golden-checked.** `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/vm-builtin-ids.txt` (the VM module plus the `IntrinsicId` list) and `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/golden/ids-<module>.txt` hold one line per definition; a reorder fails them, and `EP9_BLESS=1` rewrites them after a review that sees only appended lines.

**The VM table** (`crates/ep9_vm/src/natives.rs:702`, block 0): `%Function.prototype%` (a function that returns undefined, §20.2.3), `Function` (CreateDynamicFunction, §20.2.1.1, through the compiler hook `compile_function`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/script.rs`; an EvalError on an isolate without hooks), `Object` (new.target, then `to_object`, §20.1.1.1), `Array`, the 7 error constructors, `Object.prototype.toString`/`valueOf` (`toString` gives the builtinTag "Arguments" for an arguments object, mapped or unmapped, §20.1.3.6 step 5; M3-P2 VM-ARGS step 2), `Array.prototype.join`/`toString`/`push` (`push` is generic: a non-Array `this` takes the spec steps, §23.1.3.23), `Array.prototype.values` (also `Array.prototype[@@iterator]`, one function object), `Array.prototype.keys`/`entries` (VM ids 0.28, 0.29; M2-P4 B-ARRAY), `%ArrayIteratorPrototype%.next`, `String.prototype[@@iterator]`, `%StringIteratorPrototype%` with its `next` and `@@toStringTag` "String Iterator", `%Iterator.prototype%[@@iterator]`, the `%Iterator.prototype%[@@toStringTag]` accessor (get "Iterator", set SetterThatIgnoresPrototypeProperties; VM ids 0.31, 0.32), `Function.prototype[@@hasInstance]` (OrdinaryHasInstance; VM id 0.30), the `%Array.prototype%[@@unscopables]` object (`IntrinsicId` 44) (all three M2-P5 VM-SMALL), `Error.prototype.toString`, `%GeneratorFunction%` (through the compiler hook like `Function`), the `%GeneratorPrototype%` methods `next`, `return` and `throw`, `%AsyncFunction%` (CreateDynamicFunction of kind async through the compiler hook, M3-P1 ASYNC; its fallback prototype is `%AsyncFunction.prototype%` of GetFunctionRealm(newTarget), `abi/script.rs`), the async-generator builtins of M3-P2 ASYNCGEN (VM ids 0.33-0.40: `%AsyncGeneratorFunction%`, length 1, through the compiler hook like `Function`, its fallback prototype `%AsyncGeneratorFunction.prototype%` of GetFunctionRealm(newTarget); the `%AsyncGeneratorPrototype%` methods `next`, `return` and `throw`, length 1; `%AsyncIteratorPrototype%[@@asyncIterator]`, length 0; the `%AsyncFromSyncIteratorPrototype%` methods `next`, `return` and `throw`, length 1; with them the VM table makes `IntrinsicId` 45-49), `%ThrowTypeError%` (VM id 0.41, a method with length 0 and name "", `IntrinsicId` 63; M3-P2 VM-ARGS step 2, M3D-2: the `callee` accessor of an unmapped arguments object, §10.4.4.6 step 9, §10.2.4.1; on first use in a realm `crates/ep9_vm/src/arguments.rs` makes its `length` and `name` {w: false, e: false, c: false} and the function non-extensible; a call throws a TypeError), the global `eval` (`%eval%`, indirect eval, §19.2.1; direct eval is `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/eval.rs`), and the globals `globalThis`, `undefined`, `NaN`, `Infinity`.

**Crate layout.** `ep9_builtins` (engine dependencies ep9_vm and ep9_math only, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`) has one file per module, each with `BLOCK`, `DEFS`, `INTRINSICS`, `PROPS` and `MODULE`: object 1, function 2, array 3, number 4, boolean 5, string 6, symbol 7, math 8, global 9, promise 10, reflect 11, date 12, collections 13, json 14. `TABLE` lists them in that order, which is the property order. Its `clippy.toml` repeats the root bans (clippy reads only the nearest file) and adds `disallowed-types`: `ep9_value::Value`, `Unpacked`, `Word0` and the ep9_heap cell types (re-exports such as `ep9_vm::Value` resolve to them). `TEMPLATES.md` is the writing guide, and its code blocks are doctests. `ep9_cli` and `tools/test262` pass `TABLE`; test262 `exec` exits 2 when an isolate has no table.

**Embedders get `Local` too** (H20-Q4, filled in M3-P3): `Isolate::with_cx(|cx| ...)` runs a closure with a `Cx`; the `for<'s>` bound keeps every `Local` inside it (a `compile_fail` doctest proves the escape does not compile). `Isolate::with_cx_checkpoint(|cx| ...)` is the same scope, then a microtask checkpoint (also after a throw): a host runs a script or a call in it. The scoped forms of the old `Value` API are `Cx` methods (M0 month 2 P3, C11; `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs:896`): `run_script(unit, file) -> JsResult<Local>` (loads and runs a unit), `global_binding(name) -> JsResult<Option<Local>>` (GetBindingValue of the global environment: the lexical record first, a binding in its TDZ throws a ReferenceError, then HasProperty and `[[Get]]` on the global object, so a getter runs; `None` = unresolvable) and `display(v) -> String` (runs no JS). The scoped call is the existing `call`, and `global()` keeps its signature. `evaluate_script(source, file) -> JsResult<Local>` (M0 month 2 P3-EVAL-VM, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/script.rs:65`) compiles a source string as a sloppy Script through the compiler hook `compile_script` and runs it as `run_script` does (`this` = the global object; an EvalError on an isolate without hooks). A `compile_fail` doctest per new form shows that its `Local` cannot leave `with_cx`. `Isolate::with_module_host(resolve, load, f)` (M3-P3 EMBED-MIGRATE) puts the module host hooks in place for `import()` in all the code that runs during `f` (the Modules row). The raw `Isolate::{run_script, call, global, display}` were removed in M3-P3 (EMBED-MIGRATE). `Isolate::heap(&self) -> &Heap` also gave raw `Value`s to safe code (Codex W4-1): `Heap::alloc`, `Heap::find_cell` and `Heap::last_conservative_roots` give `CellPtr`s, and `CellPtr::to_value` makes a `Value` that no scope keeps live. M3-P3 fix round 5 put it in an `impl Isolate` block under `cfg(any(test, feature = "spike-native"))`, so the default build has no `Isolate::heap`; `Isolate::gc_stats(&self) -> GcStats` (a copy of the counters) took its 3 callers outside `ep9_vm` (`ep9_cli --gc-stats` and 2 `ep9_builtins` tests). Now no default-build `Isolate` method takes or gives a raw `Value` or a type whose safe methods give one (`Heap`, `CellPtr`, `ScratchRoots`, `Slot`), except `string_view`, which checks its `Value` (below; H20-Q4). **The embedder entry points are core** (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP step 2; fix round 5, Codex W4-3; fix round 6, RV5-F3). One test: an entry point is an `Isolate` method that runs ABI code (a `Cx` scope, JS or a `HostFn`), or that takes a hook, a function of the embedder that is called while ABI code runs. The 8 entry points are `with_cx`, `with_cx_checkpoint`, `create_realm`, `perform_microtask_checkpoint`, `take_unhandled_rejections` (the message of a reason that is an object but not an Error is ToString(reason), which can call JS or a `HostFn`; its doctest shows a `HostFn` `toString` that runs in the call), `new` (`IsolateConfig` gives it 3 kinds of hooks: the builtin tables, whose `HostFn`s ABI code calls; the `PromiseRejectionTracker`; and the `CompilerHooks`, which `Cx::evaluate_script` (`abi/script.rs`), `eval` and `Function()` call), `register_host_function` (it takes a `HostFn`) and `with_module_host` (it takes the `resolve` and `load` hooks that `import()` calls; like the compiler hooks, they get no `Cx`). With the path `ep9_vm::Isolate`, they are lines of the core golden (`embed: impl Isolate ::` lines; test `the_golden_has_the_embedder_entry_points`). `Isolate::compiler()` gives the compiler hooks back and takes none, so it is not an entry point. The types that the entry points name are core lines too (fix round 6, RV5-F2; decision M3-P3-H20-CORETYPES): the golden reads them to the end (the type closure), so `Exception`, `SourceLocation`, `IsolateError`, `IsolateConfig` (each pub field, its `Default` impl and `DEFAULT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD`), `CompileResult`, `CompileFailure`, `CompilerHooks` and the `Isolate` struct (no pub field) have core `embed:` lines with their public fields and variants, and the pub methods and trait impls (`Display`, `Error`, `Default`) of their impls in the files outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`. The ABI hook types (`BuiltinTable`, `HostFn`, `PromiseRejectionTracker`) are core lines already. Two kinds of named types are not core: `Dispatch` (the `IsolateConfig::dispatch` field) has `embed (not core):` lines, because it is the list of dispatch expansions of H15 and H15-Q2 is open (`NOT_CORE_TYPES` in the test; decision M3-P3-H20-DISPATCH: `Dispatch` stays embed, not core, as an H15 tuning type); and the types of other crates (`HeapConfig`, `HeapError`, `VerifiedUnit`, `EvalContext`, `FunctionCtorKind`) are not read, because their crates and ADRs own them. Reason: they are the only way from an embedder into the ABI, and the `for<'s>` bound of the `Cx` scopes is the Q4 guarantee; a change to one of them changes what every embedder writes. The other `Isolate` methods (`builtin_table`, `compiler`, `dispatch`, the handles, `collect_garbage`, `verify_heap`, `gc_stats`, `string_view`) are not in the core: they run no ABI code and take no hook (`collect_garbage` runs no finalizer, H12), and they belong to the embedder API of H22, H23 and H19, with those freeze points. The test is for the methods of `Isolate` only, as the code is: the extractor marks `ENTRY_POINTS` only in `impl Isolate` blocks. So `InterruptHandle::request` is not an entry point, though its callback is a function of the embedder that runs at an interrupt poll while JS runs: `request` is a method of `InterruptHandle` (H23-Q6), not of `Isolate`, and `Isolate::interrupt_handle` takes no hook (fix round 7, RV6-A2). `with_module_host` (it takes the `resolve` and `load` hooks) and `new` (it takes the `CompilerHooks` in `IsolateConfig`) are `Isolate` methods, so they still fit the test. With `request` added to `ENTRY_POINTS` (a probe, reverted), the re-blessed golden does not change and `the_golden_has_the_embedder_entry_points` fails. They are still golden lines, marked `embed (not core):` (M3-P3 fix round 2, FP2-GOLDEN-RAW). **The golden is a tripwire** (fix round 7; decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-TRIPWIRE). `h20_core.rs` reads the source text with no compiler data, and a reader of source text cannot list all of a Rust API. So the golden finds a change in the forms that it reads; it is not a complete list of the public API. Since fix round 6 (RV5-F1, RV5-S1; decision M3-P3-H20-FAILCLOSED) it reads more forms. It reads, in every `src` file of the default build: (1) every pub method and const of every inherent `impl Isolate` block, also `impl crate::Isolate` and a block with a `where` clause (the type name is read before `where`); (2) every other impl whose head names `Isolate`, as one line with its associated types, consts and method signatures: a trait impl (`impl Deref for Isolate`, `impl AsRef<Heap> for Isolate`, `impl Drop for Isolate`; none exists today) or an impl that takes it (`impl From<&Isolate> for &Heap`); (3) every free pub fn, const or static outside `abi/` whose line names `Isolate` or a raw type, in any module (today one: `export_facts`); (4) every `pub use` and `pub mod` of `lib.rs` as a `path:` line, and each re-exported item of a crate module and each pub item of a pub module (`versions`, `internals`) as a line; (5) the types that those lines name, to the end (the type closure: the type line, and the pub methods and trait impls of the impls in the files outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs` whose self type is that type), and the types that the core lines name as core lines (above). In the `abi/` files it reads every pub item and the impls whose self type is a pub `abi` type or whose trait is a pub `abi` trait. These forms are `unread:` lines, which fail `h20_core_matches_the_golden` and `the_extractor_reads_every_form`: a type alias of `Isolate` (`type IsoAlias = Isolate`, so an `impl IsoAlias` block), a `use` that renames `Isolate` or a raw type (`use crate::Isolate as Iso`), a type alias that gives a raw type another name, an impl that names `Isolate` inside a fn, block or `macro_rules!` body, a glob re-export and a module file of a pub module. The default build: an item is skipped only if a `cfg` on it, on an inline module around it or on its module in `lib.rs` is false in every default build of every target: `test`, and the features `spike-native` and `ic-stats`, which dep-guard 11 keeps out of the ep9 graph (test `only_the_off_features_are_off` reads the workspace Cargo.toml files: no crate turns them on, and the default ep9_cli build turns on `ep9_vm/jit`). A target `cfg` such as `target_pointer_width = "64"` and every other feature (`jit`, `become`) are read like a target, and the line shows the `cfg` (fix round 3, Codex C2-FP1; fix round 6, RV5-F1 M1). So a new raw producer in one of these forms (for example a raw `Value` form of `Isolate` that comes back) is a new line or an `unread:` line, and a review must bless it. A changed or removed `embed (not core):` line is not an H20 core change. Test `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` fails on any line but a `path:` or an `unread:` line (an `unread:` line fails `the_extractor_reads_every_form`) that names `Value`, `Heap`, `CellPtr`, `ScratchRoots` or `Slot` (fix round 5 read only the `Isolate` methods, and before it only `Value`; Codex W4-1), except two exact lines: `string_view`, and `IsolateError`, whose variant `Heap` holds a `HeapError`. The negative controls: `a_new_isolate_method_is_a_new_line` adds a method in `isolate.rs`, one in another file, four under target `cfg`s (a block, an inline module, a module of `lib.rs`) and two that give a `&Heap` and a `CellPtr`, and it checks that the gated `heap` block of `isolate.rs` gives no line; `each_way_to_add_a_raw_producer_fails_the_gate` makes one change at a time: a block under `cfg(feature = "jit")` and under `cfg(feature = "become")` (and no line under `cfg(feature = "ic-stats")`), a `where` clause, a type alias, a renaming `use` and a raw-type alias, `Deref`, `AsRef` and `From` impls, a free fn that `lib.rs` re-exports, a fn in a pub module and a static in a private module, a re-exported fn and an `Isolate` method that give a struct that holds a `&Heap`, a new `Exception` field, an impl in a fn body and a glob re-export. With the verifiers' forms M1-M5 in the real `src` (a probe, reverted), 9 of the 16 `h20_core` tests fail, `h20_core_matches_the_golden` among them. An `Isolate` method with a method-level `cfg` is still a line (the reader skips only gated blocks and modules), which errs on the safe side; and `the_cfg_reader_knows_the_default_build` checks the `cfg` reader on fixed input. The escape classes of the fix-round-5 review (RV5-F1 M1-M5: a block under `cfg(feature = "jit")`, a `where` clause, a type alias of `Isolate`, a trait impl for `Isolate`, a free fn that `lib.rs` re-exports or that a pub module has; RV5-S1) are now lines or `unread:` lines. Known escape classes that the golden does not read (a new raw producer or a changed member of a core type in them gives no line, and every `h20_core` test passes; the fix-round-6 verifiers proved (b)-(e) with probes, and the fix-round-7 verifier proved the RV7-1 form of (e), all reverted): (a) an item that a macro makes outside the core files: the extractor reads the source text without macro expansion and expands only the local `macro_rules!` calls of the core files (Codex C2-FP2, decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-LIMIT; a re-exported one is a line that says so, as `Atom` is); (b) a module with a `#[path]` attribute whose file is not read, and a module file in the same places: a file outside `src/`, not named `.rs`, or whose first name under `src/` is a gated module of `lib.rs` (`tests`, `native`): the directory `src/tests/` and the file `src/native.rs` (RV6-G1, RV7-3; M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 note N2: with `#[cfg(feature = "jit")] #[path = "native.rs"] mod m;` in `lib.rs`, a probe, reverted, the raw producers of `native.rs`, such as `native_target(&Isolate, Value)`, give no line and all 16 tests pass; the same form to a new file fails 9 of them); `sources()` reads every other `.rs` file under `src/`, so a `#[path]` module to such a file is read); (c) in a file outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`, a trait impl whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose self type is not a type of the closure (in `isolate.rs`, also not `Cx`), for example `impl From<Local<'s>> for Value`, `impl IntoKey<'s> for Value` or `impl From<Value> for Local<'s>` in `string.rs`; and in the `abi/` files and `builtins.rs`, a trait impl whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose trait and self type are not pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` items, such as `impl From<Local<'s>> for Value` or `impl From<Local<'s, FlatStr>> for JsStr<'static>` (RV6-G2, RV6-A1, RV7-2); (d) a type alias, a renaming `use` or an impl in a body for a type of the closure (`Exception`, `IsolateConfig`, ...): the `unread:` rules know only `Isolate` and the raw types (RV6-G3); (e) an impl whose self type is an `abi` type (`Local`, `Cx`, ...) in a file other than the `abi/` files and `builtins.rs` (`impl Cx` of `isolate.rs` is read) (RV6-G4), and an impl of a type of the closure (`Exception`, `IsolateConfig`, ...) in an `abi/` file or `builtins.rs`, unless it is a trait impl of a pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` trait: the type closure reads only the impls of the files outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`, and the `abi/` reader reads only the impls of pub `abi` and `builtins.rs` types and traits (RV7-1); (f) the types of other crates (`ep9_heap`, `ep9_value`, `ep9_bytecode`; H20-Q4-X1 names the `ep9_heap` case). In every class, an impl whose head names `Isolate` is still a line, in every file (items (1) and (2)): for example `impl crate::Isolate` or `impl From<&crate::Isolate> for crate::isolate::Exception` in `abi/mod.rs` (M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 note N1; probes, reverted: each fails 8 of the 16 tests, and `impl crate::isolate::Exception` with a pub method in `abi/mod.rs` gives no line, class (e)). So the freeze of the core depends on review too: the README §3.1 review record and the M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 H20 rows check each change for the classes (a)-(f) (for (a): each new macro that emits an impl or an item that names `Isolate` or a raw type). A complete check needs compiler data (rustdoc JSON on nightly, or a public-API tool) and is M1 work: H20-Q6. **`Isolate::string_view(&mut self, v: Value)` stays** (product call, M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP step 2), not in the core: (1) it is sound for any `v` (fix round 5, Codex W4-1): it reads `v` only if `Heap::is_cell` says that a live cell of this isolate's heap starts at it (`is_cell` finishes the lazy sweep first) and the cell's kind is a string kind, so a `Value` of a freed cell, of another `ep9_heap::Heap` or of a dropped isolate, or bits that do not start a cell, give `None`; if the memory of a freed cell now holds a new live string, the view shows that string (wrong text, but an in-bounds read of a live string); and the view borrows the isolate exclusively (H19, W4-STR-VIEW-EXCL). Before fix round 5 it had no check: Codex's probes read a freed cell (a `Value` kept in a `Vec` across a GC, zap off), read past a 16-byte cell that `Heap::alloc` made, and ended with SIGSEGV (exit 139) on a `Value` of a dropped isolate and of a dropped embedder heap. Tests: `tests::strings::string_view_reads_only_live_cells_of_its_heap` (in-crate: 64 unrooted strings, a GC with zap on and off, an interior address, an address outside the cage; with the check removed it fails on the first freed string) and the `string_view` doctest with a cell of an embedder-made heap, before and after that heap drops; (2) its two `compile_fail` doctests are the H19 check of the embedder view rule, and a `cfg` gate would make them fail to compile for another reason, so they would pass with no proof; (3) its callers hold raw `Value`s by design: the spike-native SP-Unit check (`spikes/spike-b/src/unit/checks.rs`) and an `ep9_vm` unit test. A scoped no-copy view of a `Local<FlatStr>` can come later as a part-2 addition if an embedder needs one; today `Cx::to_rust_string` copies and `Cx::code_unit_at` reads one unit.

**Public paths** (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP; closes M2-P1-FIX-NO-REEXPORT and M2-P3-ABI-REEXPORT). Each name of `ep9_vm::abi` has two public paths, `ep9_vm::abi::X` and the crate root `ep9_vm::X`, and both are in the core. The crate root re-exports the whole `abi` list: `IteratorRecord`, `ObjectClass`, `PromiseCapability`, `PromiseRejectionOperation`, `PromiseRejectionTracker` and `PromiseState`, which were only at `ep9_vm::abi`, are now at the root too (no caller changes: `ep9_builtins` uses both forms). The one exception is `ep9_vm::abi::export_facts` (V8-A13): it is not a builtin ABI name and stays at `abi::` only. A name added to `ep9_vm::abi` gets its root re-export in the same change (test `every_abi_name_has_a_crate_root_path`). The table types keep their root paths (`ep9_vm::BuiltinDef` and the others; the `builtins` module is private). `CollectionCursor` (the stack-only cursor of `collection_cursor` and `collection_next`) had no path until M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP step 2: it is `pub` in the private module `abi::collections`. Now `abi` re-exports it (one `pub use` line in `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs`, an orchestrator grant), and so does the crate root. The golden has no `path: (none)` line, and `the_golden_covers_the_core` fails if one comes back.

### Freeze table (M2-H20-PARTS)

H20 has two parts (decision M2-H20-PARTS; the table filled by M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP). A Freeze table replaces the single Freeze point (README §3.4):

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Core | `HostFn`; `Args`, `Rest`, `Local` and its markers (`JsValue`, `JsObject`, `JsFunction`, `JsArray`, `JsString`, `FlatStr`, `JsSymbol`, `JsKey`, the `Downcast` and `From` lists: the golden holds each impl that the `downcast!` and `upcast!` macros make, with its lifetimes, so an upcast keeps its scope `'s`), `Downcast`, `IntoKey`, `PropertyDescriptor`, `ScratchList`, `ArgsBuilder`, `IteratorRecord`, `PromiseCapability`, `CollectionCursor`, `ToPrimitiveHint`, `PrimitiveType`, `PromiseState`, `PromiseRejectionOperation`, `PromiseRejectionTracker`, and the types `ObjectClass` and `IntrinsicId` with their methods; `Cx` and the signature of each public `Cx` method that exists at the freeze (136 at 88998a1, and 136 after M3-P3 EMBED-MIGRATE step 2 at 89fdae1, with the scoped embedder forms `run_script`, `run_module`, `global_binding` and `display`); the embedder entry points of `Isolate` (`with_cx`, `with_cx_checkpoint`, `create_realm`, `perform_microtask_checkpoint`, `take_unhandled_rejections`, `new`, `register_host_function`, `with_module_host`; Decision, "Embedders get `Local` too"), the path `ep9_vm::Isolate`, and the types that the entry points name, to the end, with their public fields, variants, methods and trait impls (`Exception`, `SourceLocation`, `IsolateError`, `IsolateConfig` and its pub fields, `CompileResult`, `CompileFailure`, `CompilerHooks`, the `Isolate` struct; not `Dispatch`, an H15 type, and not the types of other crates; M3-P3 fix round 6, RV5-F2); the table types (`BuiltinId` with its 7 + 9 bit split, `BuiltinDef`, `BuiltinKind`, `PropDef`, `PropKey`, `PropValue`, `PropAttrs` and its constants, `ConstValue`, `IntrinsicDef`, `IntrinsicValue`, `ObjectProps`, `BuiltinModule`, `BuiltinTable`, `WellKnownSymbol`, `check_tables`); the public paths (Decision, "Public paths"); R1-R6 and the other Rules rows | M0 exit | Frozen (README §3.2, "Frozen part"): a changed or removed name, signature, field, variant, derive or path needs an approved amendment with a benchmark or gate output; its kind is rewrite, and G8 counts it after M1 exit | `crates/ep9_vm/tests/h20_core.rs` against `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/h20-core.txt` (a changed or removed line fails as a core change): a tripwire for the forms that it reads, not a complete list (Decision, "The golden is a tripwire"); review for the escape classes that it does not read (the README §3.1 review record, the M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 H20 rows); the complete check is H20-Q6 (M1); the Rules checks |
| Part 2 | The append-only lists: the `IntrinsicId` ids (0-71 now), the `BuiltinId`s of each block, the `ObjectClass` ids (0-13 now), the `Cx` method list (a new method), and the method groups in the rows below | None: the lists stay open for appends | Append only. A new id, class or method is a fill (API addition) on an Accepted part (README §3.2, "Accepted" column: orchestrator and review record), not a rewrite, so G8 does not count it. Once added, an entry is held as the core is: its id, place and signature never change | the id goldens (`crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/vm-builtin-ids.txt`, `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/golden/ids-<module>.txt`), `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/object-classes.txt`, and `h20_core` (a new line fails as an addition until it is blessed) |

The rows below are the method groups built in months 2 and 3. The methods that exist at the freeze are core lines of the golden; each group takes additions only.

| Part 2 item | After the freeze | Check |
|---|---|---|
| The `ObjectClass` list (`ep9_vm::abi::ObjectClass`, ids 0-13: ids 7-10 WeakMap, WeakSet, MapIterator, SetIterator appended by M3-P1 B-COLLECTIONS; ids 11-13 WeakRef, FinalizationRegistry, RegExpStringIterator (name "RegExp String Iterator") declared by M4-P0 for M4-P4 WEAKREF and M4-P3 REGEXP1) | append-only | golden `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/object-classes.txt`; tests `object_classes_match_the_golden`, `swapping_two_object_classes_fails_the_golden` |

| Jobs (M2-P4): the `Cx` methods `enqueue_job(callback, argument)` (HostEnqueuePromiseJob for an embedder job; a TypeError for a non-callable) and `perform_microtask_checkpoint()` (runs every queued job in FIFO order, including jobs queued meanwhile; throws when JS is on the stack, as the spec runs a checkpoint only with an empty execution-context stack; one interrupt budget unit and a poll per job; it stops at the first job that throws and returns that error, leaving the rest queued; termination drops the queue); `Isolate::with_cx_checkpoint` runs one after its scope (also after a throw); `Cx::run_script` and `with_cx` do not; `Isolate::perform_microtask_checkpoint` (both `Isolate` methods are core lines of the golden). [M4-P4, integration fixer A: each time the queue is empty, `perform_microtask_checkpoint` runs ClearKeptObjects (`clear_kept_objects`) and then the FinalizationRegistry cleanup jobs (`cleanup_finalization_registries`); jobs that a callback queues run in the same checkpoint, and a callback that throws stops it like a job that throws. With no WeakRef or FinalizationRegistry both steps do nothing.] [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer B, R-GC-P4-3: when a GC during the callbacks found more dead targets and the queue is still empty, the checkpoint runs another ClearKeptObjects and cleanup round, at most 8 in a row (`MAX_CLEANUP_ROUNDS`); the rest waits for the next checkpoint.] [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer C, P4-ORCH-8 (Codex C2-1, C2-2, B1-2): replaces the fixer B note. The checkpoint serves cleanup until none is pending, with no round cap; each cleanup callback, JS or builtin, takes one interrupt budget unit and polls (in `cleanup_finalization_registries`), so an interrupt callback runs and a termination stops a callback chain that never ends, and the cells not yet served stay pending; on its `Ok` exit the checkpoint runs ClearKeptObjects once more, after the cleanup and the jobs it queued.] [2026-10-29, M5-P1 integration fixer, from GC5 (a237d2b, 2e207d1): every exit runs it, not only `Ok`: an `Err` exit (a job or a cleanup callback threw, or a poll stopped it) runs ClearKeptObjects too (`checkpoint_exit`), and the queued jobs behind a throw stay queued. The cleanup walks of `weakref.rs` poll every 1,024 cells (`WALK_POLL_CELLS`); a walk that a termination request stops leaves the rest pending, and the checkpoint then ends at once with the termination (`Err`).] | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/jobs.rs`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs`; `tests::jobs` |

A getter or setter runs with `receiver` as `this`; a store lands on `receiver` through its `[[DefineOwnProperty]]`, so an array receiver's `length` goes through ArraySetLength (a RangeError for an invalid length). The receiver methods `collection_get`, `collection_has`, `collection_set`, `collection_delete`, `collection_clear`, `collection_size`, `collection_cursor` and `new_collection_iterator` take the `ObjectClass` of the receiver (Map, Set, WeakMap or WeakSet; `new_collection_iterator` Map or Set only, it panics for a weak class) and give a TypeError for an object of another class (RequireInternalSlot); a class that is not a collection class panics (a builtin bug). `new_collection(proto, class)` takes a collection class and panics for another. `collection_iterator_next(it, class)` takes `MapIterator` or `SetIterator` and gives a TypeError for an object of another class or for any other class. `collection_next(cursor)` and `can_be_held_weakly(v)` take no class. Keys compare by SameValueZero; an object's identity hash is H12-Q1 | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/collections.rs`; `ep9_vm` `tests::ordered_table`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/collections.rs`, `tests/realm.rs` `collections_hash_keys_across_realms` |
| Modules (M3-P2 MODULE, §16.2.1.5): the `Cx` method `run_module(unit, name, resolve, load) -> JsResult<Local>` (loads, links and evaluates the module unit `unit` as the module `name` of the current realm's module map, with the modules it imports; `resolve: &mut dyn FnMut(&str, &str) -> Result<String, String>` maps (referrer name, specifier) to a name, and `Err(text)` throws an `Error` with that text; `load: &mut dyn FnMut(&str) -> CompileResult` gives the module unit of a name not in the map, and a failure throws as a compile hook failure does; an import that no module exports or that two `export *` give is a `SyntaxError`; an evaluation error is kept and thrown again by each later import of that module; the result is undefined; with no top-level `await` in the graph it runs no microtask checkpoint, as the scoped `run_script`; with one (M3-P3 IMPORT-TLA) it runs checkpoints until the entry's evaluation settles, and a rejection is the throw). A name loads once per realm. `import()` (M3-P3) uses these hooks in the code that runs during the call, and the hooks of `Isolate::with_module_host(resolve, load, f)` (M3-P3 EMBED-MIGRATE; a core entry point) in scripts, calls and checkpoints that run during `f`; with no module host it throws the v0 not-implemented `Error` | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/module.rs`; `ep9_vm` `tests::module`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/module.rs`; the `Cx::run_module` doctest; test262 `flags: [module]` files through `tools/test262` |
| Error test (M3-P2 B-SMALL): the `Cx` method `is_error(v) -> bool` (IsError, §20.5.8.2: true for an object with `[[ErrorData]]`, false for any other value; never throws; v0 has no proxies) | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs` (`is_error`); `ep9_builtins` `tests/global.rs` `error_is_error`; test262 `built-ins/Error/isError` |
| Math.random (M4-P0, V9-D16; Math.random itself is M4-P1 MATH1): the `Cx` method `math_random(&mut self) -> f64`, the next number in [0, 1) (a multiple of 2^-52) of the isolate's xorshift128+ state (`Runtime::rng`, the V8 generator). The state is seeded per isolate from the process hash key (random from the OS, or fixed by `EP9_HASH_SEED`) and the isolate's `Runtime::id` (`runtime::rng_seed`), so each isolate of a process has its own sequence (§21.3.2.27: distinct realms, distinct sequences) and two runs with one `EP9_HASH_SEED` give the n-th isolate one sequence. Never throws, never allocates; not for cryptography | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs` (`math_random`); `ep9_vm` `tests::layout4::math_random_is_xorshift128_plus_in_0_1` and `one_hash_seed_gives_one_math_random_run` |

With the S1 heap (`HeapConfig::generational`) it frees only the young cells that nothing reaches, and the young cells it keeps become old; an S0 heap runs a full collection, as `collect_garbage` does. ## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| R1. A `Local` in a Rust local stays live across every call (CSS) | GC-stress tests: `tests::abi`, the test262 GC-stress run | M0 wk4 |
| R2. No `Local`, `Args`, `Rest`, `PropertyDescriptor`, `ArgsBuilder`, `ScratchList`, `IteratorRecord`, `JsStr` or `FlatStr` in Rust heap memory, a static, a thread_local or a boxed closure | `'s` + `!Send` (compiler); `cargo xtask gc-lint` (P2-LINT; its type list is `STACK_ONLY` in `tools/xtask/src/gc_lint.rs`, plus every type that holds one, such as `IteratorRecord`: test `a_vec_of_iterator_records_is_a_finding`); debug on-stack check in `Local::get`, the one read of a `Local` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/local.rs:182`, P2-LINT) | M0 wk4 |
| R3. Every `&mut Cx` method may allocate and run JS; a view into heap memory borrows `&Cx`, so none lives across one | borrow checker (`code_unit_at`, `string::view`) | M0 wk4 |
| R4. `Err(Throw)` only with a pending exception, `Ok` only without one; a callee's `Err` passes on unchanged | debug asserts in `call_host` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:315`); tests `*_panics_in_debug` | M0 wk4 |
| R5. A builtin creates objects in its callee's realm | `call::call_host` switches to the host function's realm (`realm::enter_realm_of`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:301`) and back after it returns or throws, so `Cx::global`, `Cx::intrinsic`, every new object and every thrown error of a builtin are its own realm's (H20-Q2); tests `ep9_vm` `tests::realm::a_host_function_runs_in_its_realm`, `ep9_builtins` `tests/realm.rs` `values_get_their_realm_prototypes` (a TypeError of `other.Object.defineProperty`, `other.Object.keys`), `ep9_builtins` `tests/realm_r5.rs` (two realms made as `$262.createRealm` makes them; 69 cases over the 14 `ep9_builtins` modules and the VM table, called from the other realm, both ways, directly or through the caller realm's `Reflect.apply`, give the callee realm's objects and errors, and 3 controls give the caller's: 146 lines, equal to node v24.22.1; evidence [M6]), and the `$262.createRealm` test262 files that pass (75 at M3-P1; 107 of the 284 files that name `$262.createRealm` at ec4b841 on aarch64-apple-darwin, M3-P4 ACCEPT-8) | M0 month 3 (M3-P1 REALMS; the two-realm test M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP) |
| R6. [[GetOwnProperty]] reports virtual properties with spec attributes: array `length` {w}, elements {w,e,c}; function `length`/`name` {c}, `prototype` {w}; String wrapper `length` and indices | `crates/ep9_vm/src/property.rs:1281`; test `virtual_property_descriptors` | M0 wk4 |

| `ScratchList` and `ArgsBuilder` nest LIFO; out of order panics | `ScratchRoots` depth check; `ArgsBuilder` scratch-top check (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:487`) | M0 wk4 |
| A Rust→JS entry or host call starts above the scratch area and restores the caller's scratch top on return; bytecode never runs with one | `call::top_of`, `restore_scratch` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:229`); test `args_builder_values_survive_gc_stress_before_the_call` (fails with the scratch scan off) | M0 wk4 |

| Every name of `ep9_vm::abi` also has a crate-root path, except `export_facts` | test `every_abi_name_has_a_crate_root_path` | M0 month 3 (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP) |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** A `Local` costs nothing (no handle scope); a host call passes 4 integer words (cx, this, args, new_target), which fits the 4 Win64 argument registers, and returns in one register. Builtins reach the VM only through `Cx`, so `forbid(unsafe_code)` holds in `ep9_builtins`. No `Vec<Local>` is ever needed: lists are a `ScratchList`, a JS array or an `ArgsBuilder`.
- **Costs.** Every builtin spells `<'s>`. R2 is a lint plus a debug check, not a type error. `call`/`apply` copy the arguments once (H20-Q3). The LIFO checks run at run time.
- **Costs of the tables.** A block holds at most 512 builtins, and 128 blocks exist; an id never moves, so a removed builtin keeps its slot. A table error shows at `Isolate::new`, not at compile time (the checks are cached per table).
- **Follow-ups.** Done in M0 wk4: gc-lint and the debug check (P2-LINT); the symbol entry points and `%Symbol.prototype%` (P2-SYM merge); accessors in `define_own_property` and `PropValue::Accessor` installs (P3-ACC); the builtins of `TEMPLATES.md` 1-10 and the `@@iterator` protocol (P3-BLTA, P3-BLTB, P3-NAT). Done in M0 month 2 P1: index access (ABI-1) and the iterator protocol (ABI-3). Done in M0 month 3 P1: H22 realms for R5 (REALMS). Done in M0 month 3 P3: the Freeze table, the public paths (with `CollectionCursor`), the two-realm R5 test and the core golden (FREEZE-PREP); the raw `Value` forms of `Isolate` removed (EMBED-MIGRATE) and H20-Q4 filled. Open: H18 element storage for the v0 limits above; the async GetIterator and GetMethod with a well-known symbol (part-2 additions for the builtins that need them). The core freezes at M0 exit (Freeze table).

Step 2 (after EMBED-MIGRATE step 2, at 89fdae1): the golden has 375 lines; the re-bless diff is 1 removed line (the `CollectionCursor` `path: (none)` line) and 9 added lines (the 6 `Isolate` entry points, the paths `ep9_vm::abi::CollectionCursor`, `ep9_vm::CollectionCursor` and `ep9_vm::Isolate`); the 136 `Cx` lines do not change; 9 tests, with the negative control `changing_an_entry_point_fails_the_golden`. Fix round 2 (Codex C-FP1, C-FP2; review FP2-GOLDEN-RAW): (1) the golden read only the input of the `upcast!` and `downcast!` macros: with the upcast impl changed to `impl<'s, 't> From<Local<'s, A>> for Local<'t, B>` (a probe, reverted) all golden tests passed, and `.into()` could take a `Local` out of `with_cx`. Now the extractor expands the local `macro_rules!` calls of the ABI files, and that probe changes the 11 `From` lines; a `compile_fail` doctest on `Local` shows the escape, and it compiles under the probe. (2) A raw `Value` method added to `impl Isolate` in `string.rs` passed the 9 tests; now it is a new `embed (not core):` line and `no_isolate_method_takes_or_gives_a_raw_value` fails. The golden has 409 lines; the re-bless diff is 2 removed lines (the `upcast!` and `downcast!` input lists) and 36 added lines (11 `From`, 7 `Downcast`, 7 `Sealed` impls; 11 `embed (not core):` lines, `string_view` one of them); no core line changes. 12 tests. (3) The R5 cases called the methods of `C` through `C.Function.prototype.call`, so `C`'s realm was entered before the host-to-host call: with the realm switch of `call::call_host` turned off only for a host caller (a probe, reverted) the test passed. Now the methods go through the caller realm's `Reflect.apply`, 8 new cases call direct-call builtins that way (one through the caller's `Function.prototype.call`), with 1 new control: 69 cases and 3 controls each way, 146 lines, equal to node v24.22.1. With that probe 38 of the 69 cases of each way print `caller`; with no switch in `call_host` at all, 68 of the 69 cases of the first way print `caller`. Fix round 5 (Codex W4-1, W4-3): the golden keeps 409 lines; the re-bless diff is 3 removed and 3 added lines: `embed (not core): ... pub fn heap(&self) -> &Heap` goes, `embed (not core): ... pub fn gc_stats(&self) -> GcStats` comes, and `new` and `take_unhandled_rejections` move from `embed (not core):` lines to core `embed:` lines (8 now); the 136 `Cx` lines do not change; 13 tests. Negative controls (each a probe, reverted): `heap` back in a default-build block fails 8 of the 13 tests, `no_isolate_method_takes_or_gives_a_raw_value` among them; `take_unhandled_rejections` out of `ENTRY_POINTS` fails 8, `the_golden_has_the_embedder_entry_points` among them; the `string_view` check removed fails `string_view_reads_only_live_cells_of_its_heap` (`Some("w4-1-unrooted-0000-0123456789")` for a freed cell, zap off); with `--features spike-native` the 3 `NoRawHeap` doctests compile (so they fail only because `heap` is gated). Fix round 6 (RV5-F1, RV5-S1, RV5-F2): the golden has 508 lines; the re-bless diff removes no line and adds 99: 14 core `embed:` lines (the types that the entry points name: `Exception`, `SourceLocation`, `IsolateError`, `IsolateConfig`, `CompileResult`, `CompileFailure`, `CompilerHooks`, the `Isolate` struct, their trait impls and `DEFAULT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD`), 58 `embed (not core):` lines (the re-exported and pub-module items and their types, such as `JsStr`, `Dispatch`, `DispatchSlot`, `CodeRegistry`, the `versions` consts, `export_facts`, and `Atom`, which a macro makes) and 27 `path:` lines (every other `pub use` and `pub mod` of `lib.rs`); the 136 `Cx` lines and every old line do not change; 16 tests. Negative controls: with each of the verifiers' forms M1-M5 in the real `src` (a `cfg(feature = "jit")` block, a `where` clause, a type alias, `Deref` and `AsRef` impls, a free fn that `lib.rs` re-exports, a free fn in `versions.rs`; each a probe, reverted), 9 of the 16 tests fail, `h20_core_matches_the_golden` among them, and `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` or `the_extractor_reads_every_form`; with each part of the fix taken out of the extractor alone (the feature reader, the `where` cut, the alias and `use` checks, the impls that name `Isolate`, the free-item rule, the re-export roots, the type closure, the body check), `each_way_to_add_a_raw_producer_fails_the_gate` fails. Fix round 7 (decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-TRIPWIRE): the fix-round-6 verifiers put a raw producer or a new core-type method in 4 more forms with no macro (RV6-G1 a `#[path]` module, RV6-G2 and RV6-A1 trait impls with a foreign self type or in `abi/`, RV6-G3 an alias, a renaming `use` or a body impl of `Exception` or `IsolateConfig`, RV6-G4 a method of `Local` or `Cx` in `string.rs`; probes, reverted): each compiles, and all 16 `h20_core` tests pass; two embedder probes took a raw `&Heap`, and a raw `Value` past `with_cx` and a GC. The extractor does not change for them: the golden is a tripwire (Decision), these are named escape classes (b)-(e), and H20-Q6 (M1) has them as its test list. The golden lines do not change (508 lines); its header comment says that it is a tripwire | M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP, 2026-10-25 |

## Validation

- `cargo test -p ep9_vm` (`tests::abi`), test262 exec and its GC-stress run on 4 targets; from P2, `cargo xtask gc-lint` in CI.
- The freeze review at M0 exit checks every row above against the built builtins (P3 templates).

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H20-Q1 | Where does `new.target` live? (= H14-Q1.) v0: the feedback slot of a `[[Construct]]` frame; `Args::new_target` reads it only when the construct bit is set | M1 | A register filled at entry (H14-Q1). **Answered by H14-Q1 (clarify 2026-10-27, M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 §4.1 task 7): not a register.** Since FRAME 2 (2026-10-21) the frame keeps new.target in its header slot fp - 3 (`slot::NEW_TARGET`), and `Args::new_target` reads that slot only when the construct bit is set, else it gives undefined (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:153-162`). The "v0" text above is the M0 wk4 state. The signature of `Args::new_target` and every core line stay as they are; no code, test or golden changes |
| H20-Q2 | The realm of a builtin with N realms (with H22-Q3, Q7) | M1 (`$262.createRealm`) | **Filled 2026-10-23 (M3-P1 REALMS): the callee's realm**, the `[[Realm]]` of the host function (`Args::callee`). The VM switches to it before the call (`call::call_host`), so a builtin needs no realm argument: the current realm of its `Cx` is its own. Tests: R5 |
| H20-Q3 | `call`/`apply` without the copy | M1 | One copy now (`call_args`, `ArgsBuilder` into the callee frame); a tail-call host path in M1, as V8's Function.prototype.call shifts the arguments and tail-calls |
| H20-Q4 | The embedder API (`Isolate`) handed out raw, unrooted `Value`s: `let v = iso.run_script(..)?; iso.collect_garbage(); iso.display(v)` read freed memory in safe code. Partly settled (wk4 P2): `Isolate::with_cx` is the `Cx` scope, and a `compile_fail` doctest shows a `Local` cannot leave it. M0 month 2 P3 (C11, the add half): the scoped forms `Cx::run_script`, `Cx::global_binding`, `Cx::display` and the existing `Cx::call`, each with a `compile_fail` doctest | M0 exit | **Filled 2026-10-25 (M3-P3; corrected in fix round 5 after Codex W4-1, W4-2, W4-3).** The claim of the first fill ("the hazard is closed" with the four raw forms gone) was false: `Isolate::heap()` still gave safe code raw `Value`s of heap cells (Codex W4-1; below). EMBED-MIGRATE moved every caller to the scoped forms (step 1) and removed `Isolate::{run_script, call, global, display}` (step 2, bfca918; `rg -n 'pub fn (run_script\|call\|global\|display)\(.*(-> Result<Value, Exception>\|-> Option<Value>\|v: Value\))' crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs` prints nothing, and `git grep -l -P '\b(iso\|isolate)\s*\.(run_script\|call\|global\|display)\('` over crates, tools/test262 and spikes finds no caller). A host runs code in `Isolate::with_cx_checkpoint` (a script, a call or a module, then a checkpoint) and gives `import()` its hooks with `Isolate::with_module_host`. Fix round 5 (W4-1) closes the producers that it found through `Isolate` for the default build: (a) `Isolate::heap` is only in the in-crate tests and the spike-native build, so the producers that Codex and the fix-round-5 map found through it (`Heap::alloc` and `alloc_shape`, the `Heap::find_cell` walk, `Heap::last_conservative_roots`, each then `CellPtr::to_value`; the handle and scratch round trips) do not compile in safe default-build code (3 `compile_fail` doctests on `NoRawHeap`; the map's probe file gives 18 E0599 errors, all `no method named heap`); `Isolate::gc_stats` gives the counters; (b) `Isolate::string_view` reads a `Value` only if it starts a live string cell of this isolate's heap (Decision, "Embedders get `Local` too"). Fix round 9 (Codex round 6, RV8-1; e17fca1, 63cf183) closes one more unchecked read, one that needs no `Isolate`, heap or `Local`, so X1-X3 did not cover it: the kind test of `Downcast` (`<JsString as Downcast>::test(bits)`, a public, doc-hidden method) read the cell header of bits that safe code chose, and `test(0x80_0000)` ended with SIGSEGV in a `forbid(unsafe_code)` crate. Now the test is a method of the private `sealed::Sealed` supertrait and takes a `sealed::Token`, which only `ep9_vm` can make (a private field and a `pub(crate)` constructor; `Local::downcast` is the caller). A `T: Downcast` bound still reaches the method, but the caller cannot make the token; two `compile_fail` doctests on `Downcast` hold the direct call (e17fca1, the head of Codex round 7) and the generic route (63cf183, after Codex round 7). The other `pub` items checked and not producers: the `Value` constructors give only immediates (`from_cell_ptr`, `from_bits_unchecked` are `unsafe`), `Local::bits` and `Local::from_bits` are `pub(crate)`, no `pub` `Cx` method takes or gives a `Value`, `HostFn` and `PromiseRejectionTracker` get `Local`s, `Exception` holds `String`s. Three named, reviewed exceptions stay: **H20-Q4-X1 (an embedder heap)**: `ep9_heap` is public, and safe code that names it can make its own `Heap` and get raw `Value`s of its cells (`Heap::new`, `Heap::alloc`, `CellPtr::to_value`). Those cells are not in any isolate's heap, no `ep9_vm` API reads them unchecked (`string_view` gives `None`, also after that heap drops: a doctest, and the map's probe that ended with SIGSEGV before the check), and to close the producer, `CellPtr::to_value` or `Heap::alloc` must become `unsafe`, an API change of `ep9_heap` (H10, H12) and not of H20. **H20-Q4-X2 (tests and spike-native)**: `cfg(test)` and the `spike-native` feature keep `Isolate::heap`, and the spike-native API gives raw `Value`s (`NativeCx::global_value`, the `native.rs` free functions); there `Heap::alloc` can make a string-kind cell with no string layout, and `string_view` reads past it (the map's probe P3: a 16-byte cell with length 10). `spike-native` is a spike feature that dep-guard 11 keeps out of the ep9 graph. **H20-Q4-X3 (a `Local` off the native stack, R2; M3-P3 fix round 6, RV5-S2; decision M3-P3-H20-X3)**: inside one `with_cx` scope, safe code can keep a `Local`, or a type that holds one (`PromiseCapability`, `IteratorRecord`), in Rust heap memory (a `Vec`, a `Box`, a struct that derives `Clone`). The GC does not see it there, so a collection can free its cell, and core `Cx` methods (`string_len`, `to_rust_string`, `code_unit_at`) then read the freed memory; `string_len` has no check, and `code_unit_at` has only a debug check (`debug_assert!(is_string(b))` in `string::units`). Fix round 7 ran the probe again (RV6-A3; a test in `ep9_cli` with `forbid(unsafe_code)`, removed after the run; aarch64-apple-darwin, debug and release, zap on and off; the zap default is on in debug, off in release): (1) 64 strings kept in a `Vec<Local<JsString>>` across a GC, then 64 new strings: with zap on, 3 of 64 `string_len` calls read the zap word and 61 read the bytes of the new strings (`0x5A5A5A5A`, "ZZZZ"), and 0 read the old text; with zap off, 61 read the new bytes and 2 read the old text; debug and release give the same counts, and the debug on-stack check does not fire; (2) 64 `Local<FlatStr>` kept in a `Vec` across two GCs, with no new strings: with zap on, all 64 `string_len` calls read the zap word; then `code_unit_at` stops at the debug `is_string` assert in a debug build (a panic), and in a release build `code_unit_at(f, 0)` gives a byte of the zap word and `code_unit_at(f, 1_000_000_000)` ends with SIGBUS (exit 138); with zap off, in both builds, the first 4 freed cells still give `string_len` 31 (the old length) and `code_unit_at(f, 0)` gives `Some(114)` (the "r" of the old text), and no check fires. R2 forbids this, and R2 is a rule, not a type guarantee: `cargo xtask gc-lint` enforces it only on the in-tree code (`crates/ep9_vm/src` and `crates/ep9_builtins/src`). On an embedder path it knows only the base list `STACK_ONLY` (`Local`, `Args`, `Rest`, `ArgsBuilder`, `ScratchList`, `PropertyDescriptor`, `JsStr`, `FlatStr`); it learns that a type holds a `Local` (`PromiseCapability`, `IteratorRecord`, `CollectionCursor`) only from the files that define it (RV6-A4). So `cargo xtask gc-lint <embedder path>` finds `Vec<Local<'s>>` but not `Vec<PromiseCapability<'s>>`, `Vec<IteratorRecord<'s>>` or `Box<PromiseCapability<'s>>` (fix round 7: 1 finding), and `cargo xtask gc-lint <embedder path> crates/ep9_vm/src` finds all 4, the debug on-stack check in `Local::get` checks only the place of the `Local` that is read, and a `Cx` method takes a `Local` by value, so the copy on the stack passes it (`cx.string_len(v[0])`), and the GC-stress runs find the rest in the tests. Embedders must keep each `Local` on the native stack and can run `cargo xtask gc-lint <their paths> crates/ep9_vm/src` (the `ep9_vm` sources are needed for the types that hold a `Local`). A type-level fix (for example a `Local` that cannot be moved into heap memory, or a handle scope) is an H10/H20 product decision for M1. This hole is older than M3-P3. The embedder entry points (8 since fix round 5, W4-3) and the types that they name (fix round 6, RV5-F2) are core lines of the golden, and every other way to the heap that the golden reads is an `embed (not core):` line (M3-P3 fix round 2; more forms since fix round 6, RV5-F1, RV5-S1; a tripwire, not a complete list, since fix round 7; Decision, "The golden is a tripwire"): the pub methods of the inherent `impl Isolate` blocks (also with a `where` clause), every other impl whose head names `Isolate` (trait impls), every free pub fn, const or static that names `Isolate` or a raw type, every re-export of `lib.rs`, every item of a pub module, and the types that these lines name, under every `cfg` but `test`, `spike-native` and `ic-stats`; an alias of `Isolate` or of a raw type, an impl that names `Isolate` in a body and a glob re-export are `unread:` lines that fail the gate. So a raw form in one of these forms that comes back is a new line or an `unread:` line, and test `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` fails on a line that names `Value`, `Heap`, `CellPtr`, `ScratchRoots` or `Slot`. Not read (the Decision lists the classes (a)-(f)): macro-made items outside the core files (M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-LIMIT), module files (also the files of `#[path]` modules) outside `src/`, not named `.rs`, under `src/tests/`, or `src/native.rs`, trait impls outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs` whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose self type is not a type of the closure (in `isolate.rs`, also not `Cx`), trait impls in `abi/` and `builtins.rs` whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose trait and self type are not pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` items, aliases and renames of the core closure types, impls of `abi` types outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`, impls of the closure types (`Exception`, `IsolateConfig`, ...) in `abi/` and `builtins.rs` (except a trait impl of a pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` trait, and an impl whose head names `Isolate`), and the types of other crates (X1). For these, the freeze depends on review; H20-Q6 is the complete check (M1). W4-2 (the golden read only the 6 entry-point names) does not hold at 65d9b10: fix round 2 (100e9bb, FP2-GOLDEN-RAW) made the golden read every pub method of every default-build `impl Isolate` block and fix round 3 (28fd8f6) added the `cfg` reader; with `pub fn global(&self, name: &str) -> Option<ep9_value::Value>` added to `isolate.rs` (a probe, reverted), 8 of the 13 `h20_core` tests fail, `h20_core_matches_the_golden` and `no_isolate_method_takes_or_gives_a_raw_value` (renamed `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` in fix round 6) among them |
| H20-Q6 | A compiler-derived public-API check (M3-P3 fix round 7, decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-TRIPWIRE). The core golden reads the source text, so it is a tripwire for the forms that it reads (Decision, "The golden is a tripwire"), not a complete list: the escape classes (a)-(f) give no line, and their freeze depends on review. A complete check needs compiler data. Its test list: each RV6 and RV7 form must give a new or changed line: a `#[path]` module to a file under `src/tests/`, outside `src/` or not named `.rs` (RV6-G1, RV7-3); `impl From<Local<'s>> for Value` in `string.rs` and in `abi/local.rs`, and `impl IntoKey<'s> for Value` and `impl From<Value> for Local<'s>` in `string.rs` (RV6-G2, RV7-2), `impl From<Local<'s, FlatStr>> for JsStr<'static>` and `impl<'s, T> From<Local<'s, T>> for Value` in `abi/local.rs` (RV6-A1); `type Ex = Exception; impl Ex`, `use crate::isolate::Exception as Ex2; impl Ex2`, an `impl crate::Exception` in a fn body and an `impl crate::IsolateConfig` in a `const _` body, each with a pub method that gives a `Value` (RV6-G3); `impl Local<'s>` and `impl Cx<'s>` with a pub method in `string.rs` (RV6-G4); `impl crate::isolate::Exception` in `abi/mod.rs` and `impl crate::isolate::IsolateConfig` in `builtins.rs`, each with a pub method that gives a `Value` (RV7-1); and a macro-made item (M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-LIMIT) | M1 (a new tool or dependency; the Cargo tables are frozen in M3) | A public-API listing from compiler data (rustdoc JSON on nightly, or a public-API tool) of `ep9_vm` in the default build, diffed against a golden; the source-text golden stays as the tripwire until then |
| H20-Q5 | A test for a primitive wrapper ([[NumberData]], [[StringData]], [[BooleanData]], [[SymbolData]], [[BigIntData]]) that does not throw. Today `ep9_builtins` json.rs `primitive_data` calls `Cx::this_primitive_value` and catches the TypeError (`Cx::catch_exception`): 10 × `JSON.stringify` of 20,000 small objects takes 0.39 s user, 0.17 s with the probe removed (M3-P1 B-JSON) | M1 (a part-2 append: part 2 has no close point, Freeze table; due date moved from "the part-2 list freeze (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP)" on 2026-10-25) | A part-2 `Cx` method `primitive_data(v) -> Option<Local>` over `wrapper::wrapper_value` (or an `Option` form of `this_primitive_value`); json.rs `primitive_data` becomes one call |

## Reopens if

- A GC-stress failure is traced to a `Local` in Rust heap memory: add a type-aware dylint lint (ABI-5 e).
- The JIT/AOT host-call stub needs more than 4 integer arguments on Win64.

Fixed in cf6e643 (`try_has_index`). It is on the H20 fixer B row 2 (the B1-1 fix wording, written by fixer C): (b), fixed in 319f303, whose text ('8 units after each full group of 8 steps, none for the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk') is true at HEAD: `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (8): the inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the end of the chain) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit, and only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss when the budget is at or below 0. So a walk of 8 steps or fewer takes no unit, the last 1 to 8 steps of a longer walk take none, a walk that uses up a budget of 1 or more runs at most 7 steps past it, and one that starts at 0 or below runs 8 steps (`call_bytecode` in call.rs takes its unit at line 496, after its `poll` at line 470, so a callee can start at 0). A miss goes to the cold path: `property::instance_of` reaches `prototype_walk_step`, which takes a unit and polls at 0 or below at its first step. The docs slice found the cleanup drain true to the code; so did row 1. On this row: no finding. (1) Codex r2 adr [medium] 'Correct the false claim that BuildInfo is opaque' is true. Row 1 (2) says '`ExecOptions`, `Preset`, `Feedback` and `BuildInfo` have private fields or only associated consts'. The right fact: `ExecOptions` (fields `preset` and `feedback`, both private), `Preset(u8)` and `Feedback(u8)` (private tuple fields; associated consts) are opaque. `BuildInfo` has 2 public fields, `pub features: &'static [&'static str]` and `pub preset: &'static str` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/exec.rs:159-165`, no `#[non_exhaustive]`; `h20-core.txt:288`, an `embed (not core)` line), as plan v10 §2.4 step 7 names it (`build_info() -> BuildInfo { features, preset }`). So a caller can build and fully destructure a `BuildInfo`, and adding a field changes that part-2 line, which is not an append (README §3.4: 'an existing value never changes'); FREEZE-M1 reviews it. Sort (b) (M5-P0-CODEX-R2): a false statement in this phase's record, not in the code or the golden. Row 1 stays as written (rule 21 (d)); this row states the fact; no code change.
